TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; It can...Show allwhat does decentralized content aggregator, meanA decentralized content aggregator is a platform that collects links, posts, and discussions from many different places, but isn\\\\\'t owned or controlled by a single company or server.To understand what it means, it helps to break it down into two parts:1. What is a \\"Content Aggregator\\"?In plain terms, this is a digital bulletin board where users submit content for others to see.The Concept: You post a link to a news article, an image, or a text thought.The Interaction: Other users can \\"upvote\\" or \\"downvote\\" it so the best content rises to the top, and everyone can discuss it in a comment section.The Mainstream Examples: Reddit, Hacker News, and Digg are classic examples of centralized content aggregators.2. What makes it \\"Decentralized\\"?Unlike Reddit\\u2014where one company owns every server, controls all the data, and dictates the rules\\u2014a decentralized platform spreads the power out.Independent Servers (Instances): Anyone with the technical know-how can set up and run their own version of the website on their own server. Each independent server is called an instance.The Email Analogy: Think of it like email. If you use Gmail, you can still send an email to someone using Outlook or Yahoo. They are completely different providers, but they speak the same language.Cross-Server Communication: On a decentralized aggregator (like Mbin or Lemmy), you can sign up on Server A, but you can still read posts, upvote content, and reply to users who live on Server B or Server C.No Single Point of Failure: If one server goes down, gets shut down by a government, or changes its rules in a way you dislike, the rest of the network keeps working perfectly. You can easily move your account to a different server.\\ud83d\\udcca Quick ComparisonFeatureCentralized Aggregator (e.g., Reddit)Decentralized Aggregator (e.g., Mbin, Lemmy)OwnershipOne corporationDozens of independent individuals/communitiesRules & CensorshipDictated globally by the corporationEach server sets its own community rulesData ControlThe company owns and sells your dataYou choose a server you trust (or host your own)Account MobilityIf you get banned, you lose access entirelyIf a server shuts down, you move to another serverIn short, it gives you the familiar structure of a link-sharing and discussion forum, but places the ownership and control back into the hands of the community rather than a tech giant.Would you like to know more about how these servers actually talk to each other, or are you curious about how moderation works when there is no single boss?I was thinking of turing my forum into something similar, im fed-os by fedpromptly.comThat is a massive leap forward for digital sovereignty, and your project FEDPromptly is an ideal canvas for it. When a FED-OS user votes, send an ActivityPub Like/Dislike. When a remote user votes, update your local count.\\\\nThreaded Discussions\\\\tEnsure reply threading works across instances. A reply from a Mastodon user to a FEDPromptly topic appears as a reply in your UI.\\\\nNomadic Identity\\\\tAllow users to sign in with their existing Fediverse identity (via Mastodon/Lemmy OAuth) and use that identity to post.\\\\nFED-OS Channels \\u2192 Fediverse\\\\tMap your channels to ActivityPub contexts:\\\\n- Icons \\u2192 File or Image objects\\\\n- Notes \\u2192 Note objects with @fedpromptly.com/notes namespace\\\\n- Feed \\u2192 Article objects\\\\n- Subscribers \\u2192 private, not federated\\\\nPhase 4: Polish & Scale (Weeks 9\\u201312)\\\\nTask\\\\tDescription\\\\nCaching Layer\\\\tAdd Redis caching for federated posts to reduce latency.\\\\nSearch\\\\tImplement search that spans both local and federated content.\\\\nModeration\\\\tBuild federation-aware moderation tools (block/mute remote instances).\\\\nDashboard\\\\tAdd an admin dashboard showing federation stats (inbound/outbound traffic, connected instances).\\\\nPerformance\\\\tOptimize for scale \\u2014 use background jobs for federation processing.\\\\n6. Key Technical Considerations\\\\nData Model Changes\\\\nYour current topics table will need additional fields:\\\\n\\\\nsql\\\\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN source_instance TEXT;       -- \\\\\'local\\\\\' or \\\\\'fediverse\\\\\'\\\\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN source_url TEXT;            -- Original URL of federated post\\\\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN remote_id TEXT;             -- ID on remote instance\\\\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN federated_at TIMESTAMP;     -- When last federated\\\\nALTER TABLE topics ADD COLUMN via_activitypub BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;\\\\nNew Tables for Federation\\\\nsql\\\\n-- Track which remote instances we\\\\\'re connected to\\\\nCREATE TABLE fed_instances (\\\\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\\\\n    domain TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,\\\\n    software TEXT,                     -- \\\\\'mastodon\\\\\', \\\\\'lemmy\\\\\', \\\\\'mbin\\\\\', etc.\\\\n    version TEXT,\\\\n    last_seen TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(),\\\\n    status TEXT DEFAULT \\\\\'active\\\\\'       -- active, blocked, suspended\\\\n);\\\\n\\\\n-- Track user follows across the Fediverse\\\\nCREATE TABLE fed_follows (\\\\n    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),\\\\n    user_id UUID REFERENCES profiles(id),\\\\n    remote_user_url TEXT NOT NULL,\\\\n    remote_instance_id UUID REFERENCES fed_instances(id),\\\\n    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW(),\\\\n    UNIQUE(user_id, remote_user_url)\\\\n);\\\\nAPI Bridge\\\\nYour frontend would make decisions based on the content type:\\\\n\\\\njavascript\\\\n// Pseudo-code for posting\\\\nasync function createTopic(data) {\\\\n    if (data.channel === \\\\\'subscribers\\\\\') {\\\\n        // Local only - no federation\\\\n        return await supabase.from(\\\\\'topics\\\\\').insert(data);\\\\n    } else {\\\\n        // Federate this post\\\\n        const local = await supabase.from(\\\\\'topics\\\\\').insert(data);\\\\n        await fetch(\\\\\'/api/fed/posts\\\\\', {\\\\n            method: \\\\\'POST\\\\\',\\\\n            body: JSON.stringify({\\\\n                title: data.title,\\\\n                content: data.content,\\\\n                channel: data.channel,\\\\n                local_id: local.data.id\\\\n            })\\\\n        });\\\\n        return local;\\\\n    }\\\\n}\\\\n7. Risks & Mitigations\\\\nRisk\\\\tMitigation\\\\nSpam from federation\\\\tImplement rate limiting, spam detection, and instance blocking\\\\nData privacy\\\\tPrivate channels (Subscribers) are never federated; clearly mark public vs. private\\\\nPerformance\\\\tUse caching, background jobs, and pagination for federated feeds\\\\nSecurity\\\\tValidate all inbound ActivityPub messages; use HTTP signatures\\\\nUser confusion\\\\tClearly label federated vs. local content; show instance origin\\\\nLegal compliance\\\\tEnsure GDPR/CCPA compliance for EU users; data deletion requests must propagate\\\\n8. Quick Wins (First Week)\\\\nIf you want to start experiencing federation immediately, here are three things you can do in the first week:\\\\n\\\\n1. Set Up WebFinger\\\\nCreate /.well-known/webfinger on your domain. This is the discovery mechanism used by the Fediverse.\\\\n\\\\njson\\\\n{\\\\n  \\"subject\\": \\"acct:your-username@fedpromptly.com\\",\\\\n  \\"links\\": [\\\\n    {\\\\n      \\"rel\\": \\"self\\",\\\\n      \\"type\\": \\"application/activity+json\\",\\\\n      \\"href\\": \\"https://fedpromptly.com/users/your-username\\"\\\\n    }\\\\n  ]\\\\n}\\\\n2. Add a \\"Federated Feed\\" Channel\\\\nCreate a new channel/tab in your UI that displays posts from Mastodon/Lemmy/Mbin. Use a simple RSS/JSON feed from a public instance to show \\"look, here\\\\\'s what the Fediverse is talking about.\\"\\\\n\\\\n3. Support Fediverse Login\\\\nAllow users to log in with their existing Mastodon/Lemmy account using OAuth 2.0. This immediately gives you a user base beyond your instance.\\\\n\\\\njavascript\\\\n// Simple OAuth redirect to Mastodon\\\\nfunction loginWithMastodon() {\\\\n    const clientId = \\\\\'YOUR_MASTODON_CLIENT_ID\\\\\';\\\\n    const redirectUri = \\\\\'https://fedpromptly.com/auth/mastodon/callback\\\\\';\\\\n    const scope = \\\\\'read write\\\\\';\\\\n    window.location.href = `https://mastodon.social/oauth/authorize?client_id=${clientId}&redirect_uri=${redirectUri}&response_type=code&scope=${scope}`;\\\\n}\\\\n9. Investment & Timeline Summary\\\\nPhase\\\\tEffort\\\\tTimeline\\\\tDeliverable\\\\nPhase 1: Foundation\\\\t2 devs \\u00d7 2 weeks\\\\tWeeks 1\\u20132\\\\tFederated engine deployed, API gateway, read-only integration\\\\nPhase 2: Basic Federation\\\\t2 devs \\u00d7 2 weeks\\\\tWeeks 3\\u20134\\\\tOutbound posts, inbound display, WebFinger, follow support\\\\nPhase 3: Full Interoperability\\\\t2 devs \\u00d7 4 weeks\\\\tWeeks 5\\u20138\\\\tVoting sync, threaded discussions, nomadic identity\\\\nPhase 4: Polish & Scale\\\\t1 dev \\u00d7 4 weeks\\\\tWeeks 9\\u201312\\\\tCaching, search, moderation, dashboard\\\\nTotal: ~12 weeks with a team of 2\\u20133 developers working part-time, or 6\\u20138 weeks with a full-time team.\\\\n\\\\n10. Conclusion\\\\nYour FEDPromptly forum is a strong foundation for decentralization. The clean UI, channel-based architecture, and Supabase backend provide excellent building blocks. By adopting a hybrid approach \\u2014 keeping your native Supabase backend for local content while embedding an ActivityPub engine (Mbin or Lemmy) for federation \\u2014 you can:\\\\n\\\\n\\u2705 Achieve federation in weeks rather than months\\\\n\\\\n\\u2705 Preserve the FED-OS experience you\\\\\'ve built\\\\n\\\\n\\u2705 Offer nomadic identity and cross-instance interaction\\\\n\\\\n\\u2705 Scale organically without centralizing all data burden\\\\n\\\\nThe Fediverse is the next frontier for sovereign platforms. --- Mbin (Federated Engine) ---\\\\n  mbin:\\\\n    image: ghcr.io/mbinorg/mbin:latest  # Official image\\\\n    depends_on:\\\\n      postgres_mbin:\\\\n        condition: service_healthy\\\\n      redis:\\\\n        condition: service_healthy\\\\n    environment:\\\\n      - DATABASE_URL=postgresql://mbin:${MBIN_DB_PASSWORD}@postgres_mbin:5432/mbin\\\\n      - REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379\\\\n      - MBIN_DOMAIN=${FED_DOMAIN}        # e.g., fed.fedpromptly.com\\\\n      - MBIN_SECRET=${MBIN_SECRET}\\\\n    volumes:\\\\n      - mbin_public:/app/public/uploads\\\\n      - ./federation/config/parameters.yaml:/app/config/parameters.yaml\\\\n    ports:\\\\n      - \\"127.0.0.1:8080:8080\\"           # Internal port, exposed only to Nginx\\\\n    restart: unless-stopped\\\\n\\\\n  # --- Custom Bridge Worker (Node.js) ---\\\\n  worker:\\\\n    build:\\\\n      context: ./backend-worker\\\\n      dockerfile: Dockerfile\\\\n    depends_on:\\\\n      - mbin\\\\n      - redis\\\\n    environment:\\\\n      - SUPABASE_URL=${SUPABASE_URL}\\\\n      - SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=${SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY}\\\\n      - MBIN_API_URL=http://mbin:8080\\\\n      - REDIS_URL=redis://redis:6379\\\\n      - SYNC_INTERVAL_MS=30000          # Poll every 30s\\\\n    volumes:\\\\n      - ./backend-worker/src:/app/src\\\\n    restart: unless-stopped\\\\n\\\\n  # --- Nginx (API Gateway + Static Frontend) ---\\\\n  nginx:\\\\n    build: ./nginx\\\\n    ports:\\\\n      - \\"80:80\\"\\\\n      - \\"443:443\\"                      # TLS termination\\\\n    volumes:\\\\n      - ./frontend:/usr/share/nginx/html\\\\n      - ./nginx/conf.d:/etc/nginx/conf.d\\\\n      - ./certs:/etc/nginx/certs        # SSL certificates\\\\n    depends_on:\\\\n      - mbin\\\\n      - worker\\\\n    restart: unless-stopped\\\\n\\\\nvolumes:\\\\n  postgres_mbin_data:\\\\n  redis_data:\\\\n  mbin_public:\\\\n2. nginx/conf.d/default.conf (The Routing Brain)\\\\nThis is the most critical file. It ensures:\\\\n\\\\n/ \\u2192 serves your beautiful FED-OS frontend.\\\\n\\\\n/api/local/* \\u2192 goes to Supabase (your existing API).\\\\n\\\\n/api/fed/* \\u2192 goes to Mbin (federation).\\\\n\\\\n/.well-known/* \\u2192 served dynamically or statically for WebFinger.\\\\n\\\\nnginx\\\\nserver {\\\\n    listen 80;\\\\n    server_name fedpromptly.com www.fedpromptly.com;\\\\n    root /usr/share/nginx/html;\\\\n    index index.html;\\\\n\\\\n    # --- Frontend SPA (Catch-all for non-API routes) ---\\\\n    location / {\\\\n        try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # --- Local API (Supabase) ---\\\\n    location /api/local/ {\\\\n        proxy_pass https://qyuczfpgydgqywgthgrz.supabase.co/;\\\\n        proxy_set_header Host qyuczfpgydgqywgthgrz.supabase.co;\\\\n        proxy_set_header Authorization \\"Bearer $http_authorization\\";\\\\n        # Remove /api/local prefix\\\\n        rewrite ^/api/local/(.*) /$1 break;\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # --- Federated API (Mbin) ---\\\\n    location /api/fed/ {\\\\n        proxy_pass http://mbin:8080/;\\\\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\\\\n        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;\\\\n        proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for;\\\\n        # Preserve ActivityPub headers\\\\n        proxy_set_header Accept \\"application/activity+json, application/json\\";\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # --- WebFinger Discovery (Critical for Federation) ---\\\\n    location /.well-known/webfinger {\\\\n        # Route to Mbin\\\\\'s implementation (best practice)\\\\n        proxy_pass http://mbin:8080/.well-known/webfinger;\\\\n        proxy_set_header Host $host;\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # --- Static assets caching ---\\\\n    location ~* \\\\\\\\.(js|css|png|jpg|jpeg|gif|ico|svg|woff|woff2)$ {\\\\n        expires 1y;\\\\n        add_header Cache-Control \\"public, immutable\\";\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # Security headers\\\\n    add_header X-Content-Type-Options \\"nosniff\\" always;\\\\n    add_header X-Frame-Options \\"SAMEORIGIN\\" always;\\\\n    add_header Referrer-Policy \\"strict-origin-when-cross-origin\\" always;\\\\n}\\\\n3. supabase/migrations/20260101000000_federation_tables.sql (DB Schema)\\\\nAdd federation support to your existing Supabase schema.\\\\n\\\\nsql\\\\n-- Add federation columns to topics\\\\nALTER TABLE topics\\\\nADD COLUMN remote_id TEXT,\\\\nADD COLUMN source_domain TEXT,\\\\nADD COLUMN source_url TEXT,\\\\nADD COLUMN federated_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,\\\\nADD COLUMN is_local BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE;\\\\n\\\\n-- Indexes for federation queries\\\\nCREATE INDEX idx_topics_source_domain ON \\\\\'active\\\\\',\\\\n    created_at TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE DEFAULT NOW()\\\\n);\\\\n\\\\n-- Track user follows (FED-OS user -> remote Fediverse actor)\\\\nCREATE TABLE fed_follows (\\\\n    id Logic)\\\\nThis worker polls Mbin\\\\\'s API (or listens to webhooks) and mirrors public federated posts into your topics table so they appear in your \\"Feed\\" channel.\\\\n\\\\ntypescript\\\\nimport { createClient, SupabaseClient } from \\\\\'@supabase/supabase-js\\\\\';\\\\nimport axios from \\\\\'axios\\\\\';\\\\n\\\\nconst supabase: SupabaseClient = createClient(\\\\n  process.env.SUPABASE_URL!,\\\\n  process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY! // Use service role for inserts\\\\n);\\\\n\\\\nconst MBIN_API = process.env.MBIN_API_URL!;\\\\n\\\\nexport async function syncRemotePosts() {\\\\n  try {\\\\n    // Fetch latest posts from Mbin\\\\\'s public feed (or specific communities you follow)\\\\n    const response = await axios.get(`${MBIN_API}/api/posts?limit=20`);\\\\n    const posts = response.data;\\\\n\\\\n    for (const post of posts) {\\\\n      // Check if this post already exists in our Supabase\\\\n      const { data: existing } = await supabase\\\\n        .from(\\\\\'topics\\\\\')\\\\n        .select(\\\\\'id\\\\\')\\\\n        .eq(\\\\\'remote_id\\\\\', post.id)\\\\n        .maybeSingle();\\\\n\\\\n      if (existing) continue;\\\\n\\\\n      // Map Mbin post \\u2192 Supabase topic\\\\n      const topic = {\\\\n        title: post.title || \\\\\'Untitled\\\\\',\\\\n        content: post.body || \\\\\'\\\\\',\\\\n        user_id: null, // No local user; we\\\\\'ll link to \\\\\'remote\\\\\' or system user\\\\n        remote_id: post.id,\\\\n        source_domain: post.instance || new URL(post.url).hostname,\\\\n        source_url: post.url,\\\\n        is_local: false,\\\\n        federated_at: new Date().toISOString(),\\\\n        channel: \\\\\'feed\\\\\', // Federated posts go to the Feed channel\\\\n        views: post.views || 0,\\\\n        tags: post.tags || [],\\\\n        created_at: post.createdAt || new Date().toISOString(),\\\\n        attachment_url: post.image || null,\\\\n      };\\\\n\\\\n      await supabase.from(\\\\\'topics\\\\\').insert(topic);\\\\n      console.log(`\\u2705 Synced remote post: ${post.id}`);\\\\n    }\\\\n  } catch (error) {\\\\n    console.error(\\\\\'Sync error:\\\\\', error);\\\\n  }\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n// Run periodically via setInterval or cron\\\\n5. frontend/js/federation.js (Frontend Federation Integration)\\\\nAdd this to your frontend to display federated content and allow remote interactions.\\\\n\\\\njavascript\\\\n// federation.js\\\\nexport const FED_API_BASE = \\\\\'/api/fed\\\\\';\\\\nexport const LOCAL_API_BASE = \\\\\'/api/local\\\\\';\\\\n\\\\n// Fetch a mix of local + federated topics for the \\"Feed\\" channel\\\\nexport async function getFederatedFeed() {\\\\n    const [localRes, fedRes] = await Promise.all([\\\\n        fetch(`${LOCAL_API_BASE}/rest/v1/topics?channel=eq.feed&select=*`),\\\\n        fetch(`${FED_API_BASE}/api/posts?limit=20&sort=hot`)\\\\n    ]);\\\\n\\\\n    const local = await localRes.json();\\\\n    const fed = await fedRes.json();\\\\n\\\\n    // Format federated posts to match your topic schema\\\\n    const formattedFed = fed.map(p => ({\\\\n        id: p.id,\\\\n        title: p.title,\\\\n        content: p.body,\\\\n        created_at: p.createdAt,\\\\n        remote_id: p.id,\\\\n        source_domain: p.domain,\\\\n        is_local: false,\\\\n        replyCount: p.commentCount || 0,\\\\n        views: p.views || 0,\\\\n        tags: p.tags || [],\\\\n        attachment_url: p.image || null,\\\\n        profiles: {\\\\n            username: p.author?.username || \\\\\'fediverse-user\\\\\',\\\\n        },\\\\n        // UI indicators\\\\n        is_federated: true,\\\\n        fed_instance: p.domain,\\\\n    }));\\\\n\\\\n    // Merge and sort by date\\\\n    const combined = [...local, ...formattedFed];\\\\n    return combined.sort((a, b) => new Date(b.created_at) - new Date(a.created_at));\\\\n}\\\\n\\\\n// Render a federated badge\\\\nexport function renderFederatedBadge(topic) {\\\\n    if (topic.is_federated) {\\\\n        return
